### DSH + LongMemory — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/dsh_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/dsh_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-23) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/dsh-longmemory)

> Upstream ships prereleases only (alpha/rc every few days); the pin is an npm version whose git tag is dsh-v<version>. LongMemory is pinned in its own template repo. Sign-in is a generated DSH_GATE_PASSWORD, not the launch token: DSH mints that token with randomBytes() per process and it cannot be preset, so the gateway redeems it behind the edge gate and the deployer only sees a browser password prompt. The gate and auto sign-in are interlocked — clearing the password disables both. The gate and the redirect must live in a Caddy `route`, not a `handle`, or `redir` sorts ahead of `basic_auth` and an anonymous GET / is answered 302 with the live token; test-gateway.sh pins that case. See guide §5.22.

- [v2026.9.13](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.13) 2026-09-24
  > `mise run` can now export OpenTelemetry traces and logs (experimental), and experimental daemon providers let several projects and worktrees share one PostgreSQL, CockroachDB, or NATS server, each with its own database or account. Lockfiles no longer switch backends on their own when the registry moves a tool: the new `mise backends switch` command does it when you ask. `[dotfiles]` and `[bootstrap.files]` can now remove files and manage permissions, and `mise bootstrap unapply` removes what a module set up. The experimental `pkgx:` backend has been removed.
  > 
  > ## Highlights
  > - **Observability an …
- [v2026.9.12](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.12) 2026-09-20
  > The experimental `[daemons]` system grows substantially: tasks can declare the daemons they need, `[daemon_groups]` selects subsets of a project's services, `port = "auto"` and stable `<NAME>_URL` hostnames let several git worktrees run the same stack side by side, and CockroachDB, NATS, and SpiceDB join the PostgreSQL and Redis presets. Outside daemons, `[bootstrap.packages]` gains `scoop:` and `zypper:` managers, `mise install --system` elevates with sudo only for the final publish step, official mise images are published to GHCR and Docker Hub, and a run of `brew-cask` fixes lets many more …
- [v2026.9.11](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.11) 2026-09-17
  > A new `macos-app` bootstrap manager installs `.app` bundles from a pinned URL and checksum when no Homebrew cask exists, task templates now compose `usage` flags and can be extended from file tasks, and Swift on Linux picks its distro build from swift.org's release index instead of a hard-coded map that 404'd on arm64 and on newer Fedora, Amazon Linux, and Arch hosts. Install failures also become far more actionable: errors name the `minimum_release_age` cutoff that hid every version, the child's last stderr line, or the shared libraries a Swift toolchain cannot load.
  >

  > - **Apps w …
- [v2026.9.10](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.10) 2026-09-16
  > New settings let mise manage a tool without claiming its command names (`shims.exclude`) and approve non-registry npm dependencies (`allow_exotic_deps`), `mise dot pull` can decide every sharing conflict at once, and vfox plugin hooks gain `cmd.stream` plus a working `cmd.exec` timeout. On the fix side, `mise run` now survives Ctrl-C on Windows instead of orphaning `cmd.exe`, fish shells launched through shims start much faster with correct PATH order, and several task-resolution, lockfile, and Homebrew cask bugs are corrected.
  > 
  > ## Highlights
  > - **Control over what mise puts on PATH and what it …
- [v2026.9.9](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.9) 2026-09-15
  > The dotfiles history watcher no longer records files as deleted when a checkpoint and a sync compose snapshots at the same time, `mise dot track --encrypt` enrolls a file with encrypted history from its first checkpoint, `mise bootstrap --adopt --replace-history` discards unrelated local history in one shot, and `pypi:` tools gain lock-aware `with`, `expose`, and `dependency_prereleases` options. Also fixed: `packslip:` installs from private GitHub repositories, stale history watchers after upgrading, global npm tools being reinstalled under `lockfile = true`, and the `--` separator in activat …
- [v2026.9.8](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.8) 2026-09-14
  > Dotfile management moves to the top level as `mise dot`, Homebrew bootstrap installs run their download, extraction, and linking stages concurrently, and several install paths are corrected: embedded aube reputation gates now report the real reason and honor `--yes`, PyPI tools fall back to version-only installs when a dependency graph cannot be built, lazy tools no longer trigger `missing:` warnings, and lockfiles no longer resurrect disabled backends.

Newer releases (2):
- [v1.4.1](https://github.com/EverMind-AI/EverOS/releases/tag/v1.4.1) 2026-09-24
  > **A fresh install works again.** The `openai` SDK released its 3.x line on
  > 2026-09-24; an unconstrained install picked it up together with an httpx
  > pre-release, and every LLM and embedding call failed before reaching the
  > network. This release pins the SDK to the 2.x line the project is tested
  > against. Nothing else changed since 1.4.0.
  > 
  > ## Fixed
  > 
  > - **`openai` is pinned below 3.** Fresh installs from PyPI resolved
  >   `openai 3.19.2`, whose client raises `AttributeError: module 'httpx' has no
  >   attribute 'Timeout'` on every request, so memorize and hybrid / vector
  >   search returned 500. Existing e …
- [v1.4.0](https://github.com/EverMind-AI/EverOS/releases/tag/v1.4.0) 2026-09-24
  > **EverOS runs natively on Windows, and dense search stops scanning the whole
  > table.** `pip install everos` on a stock Windows 11 machine now works with no
  > manual steps beyond an LLM key; the unit suite runs on Windows in CI. Every
  > vector column gets an IVF_FLAT index once it holds enough rows, built and
  > maintained by the cascade worker, so hybrid search no longer reads the entire
  > column per query. A ten-hour Windows soak drove the rest of this release: a
  > second process can no longer write the index behind a running server, watcher
  > events for one file commit in the order they arrived, and a tra …

### Marmot — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/marmot_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/marmot_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/marmot)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image plus su-exec. The entrypoint replaces Marmot's built-in admin:admin against a loopback-only listener before the public port opens, because upstream ships that credential and only tells you to change it after first login — on a public domain that would leave the catalog, the REST API and the MCP endpoint open to whoever tries the obvious pair. Image tags are semver WITHOUT a v prefix; the git tag has one, and upstream also publishes -previewN tags that the tag_pattern deliberately excludes. MARMOT_SERVER_ENCRYPTION_KEY must base64-decode to exactly 32 bytes, which ${{secret(N)}} cannot produce, so the key is derived as base64(sha256(MARMOT_ENCRYPTION_SEED)). Plugins (31 of them, ~800 MB, no way to narrow the list) install from ghcr.io at every startup, so MARMOT_PLUGINS_DIR points at the volume to pay for it once. MIT. See guide §5.34.

- [v1.11.0](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/pipecat/releases/tag/v1.11.0) 2026-09-17
  > ### Added
  > 
  > - Added `BaseAudioResampler.flush()` and `BaseAudioResampler.reset()`, so callers can mark stream boundaries themselves rather than relying on `SOXRStreamAudioResampler`'s inactivity timeout. `flush()` returns the audio the resampler is still holding, for a stream that has ended; `reset()` discards it, for one that was abandoned. Both default to no-ops for resamplers that keep no state between calls.
  >   (PR [#5386](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/pipecat/pull/5386))

  > ### Added
  > 
  > - `SmallestTTSService` now uses Smallest AI's continuation API: text fragments within the same LLM turn share a `context_id` so the server joins them into one continuous generation instead of resetting prosody on each request.   >   (PR [#5626](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/pipecat/pull/5626))

### Scrumboy — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/scrumboy_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/scrumboy_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/scrumboy-1)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image plus curl and tini. The entrypoint claims the Owner account against a loopback-only listener before the public port opens, because Scrumboy gives ownership to the first caller of /api/auth/bootstrap and serves anonymous reads and writes until then. Image tags are semver WITHOUT a v prefix; the git tag has one. AGPL-3.0. See guide \u00a75.29.

- [v2026.9.13](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.13) 2026-09-24
  > `mise run` can now export OpenTelemetry traces and logs (experimental), and experimental daemon providers let several projects and worktrees share one PostgreSQL, CockroachDB, or NATS server, each with its own database or account. Lockfiles no longer switch backends on their own when the registry moves a tool: the new `mise backends switch` command does it when you ask. `[dotfiles]` and `[bootstrap.files]` can now remove files and manage permissions, and `mise bootstrap unapply` removes what a module set up. The experimental `pkgx:` backend has been removed.
  > 
  > ## Highlights
  > - **Observability an …
- [v2026.9.12](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.12) 2026-09-20
  > The experimental `[daemons]` system grows substantially: tasks can declare the daemons they need, `[daemon_groups]` selects subsets of a project's services, `port = "auto"` and stable `<NAME>_URL` hostnames let several git worktrees run the same stack side by side, and CockroachDB, NATS, and SpiceDB join the PostgreSQL and Redis presets. Outside daemons, `[bootstrap.packages]` gains `scoop:` and `zypper:` managers, `mise install --system` elevates with sudo only for the final publish step, official mise images are published to GHCR and Docker Hub, and a run of `brew-cask` fixes lets many more …
- [v2026.9.11](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.11) 2026-09-17
  > A new `macos-app` bootstrap manager installs `.app` bundles from a pinned URL and checksum when no Homebrew cask exists, task templates now compose `usage` flags and can be extended from file tasks, and Swift on Linux picks its distro build from swift.org's release index instead of a hard-coded map that 404'd on arm64 and on newer Fedora, Amazon Linux, and Arch hosts. Install failures also become far more actionable: errors name the `minimum_release_age` cutoff that hid every version, the child's last stderr line, or the shared libraries a Swift toolchain cannot load.
  > 
  > ## Highlights
  > - **Apps w …
- [v2026.9.10](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.10) 2026-09-16
  > New settings let mise manage a tool without claiming its command names (`shims.exclude`) and approve non-registry npm dependencies (`allow_exotic_deps`), `mise dot pull` can decide every sharing conflict at once, and vfox plugin hooks gain `cmd.stream` plus a working `cmd.exec` timeout. On the fix side, `mise run` now survives Ctrl-C on Windows instead of orphaning `cmd.exe`, fish shells launched through shims start much faster with correct PATH order, and several task-resolution, lockfile, and Homebrew cask bugs are corrected.
  > 
  > ## Highlights
  > - **Control over what mise puts on PATH and what it …
- [v2026.9.9](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.9) 2026-09-15
  > The dotfiles history watcher no longer records files as deleted when a checkpoint and a sync compose snapshots at the same time, `mise dot track --encrypt` enrolls a file with encrypted history from its first checkpoint, `mise bootstrap --adopt --replace-history` discards unrelated local history in one shot, and `pypi:` tools gain lock-aware `with`, `expose`, and `dependency_prereleases` options. Also fixed: `packslip:` installs from private GitHub repositories, stale history watchers after upgrading, global npm tools being reinstalled under `lockfile = true`, and the `--` separator in activat …
- [v2026.9.8](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.8) 2026-09-14
  > Dotfile management moves to the top level as `mise dot`, Homebrew bootstrap installs run their download, extraction, and linking stages concurrently, and several install paths are corrected: embedded aube reputation gates now report the real reason and honor `--yes`, PyPI tools fall back to version-only installs when a dependency graph cannot be built, lazy tools no longer trigger `missing:` warnings, and lockfiles no longer resurrect disabled backends.

> Thin wrapper around upstream's published multi-arch image; the pin is the image tag = git tag v<version>. Upstream releases roughly weekly and sha-pins every build input. MIT glue over an AGPL-3.0 web UI (CloudCLI) and Anthropic's proprietary Claude Code. See guide §5.25.

### QwenPaw — 🟠 review

Template repo [RockinPaul/qwenpaw_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/qwenpaw_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/qwenpaw-1)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image, used unmodified - only env vars and an entrypoint. Requires a login upstream leaves optional, and deliberately runs NO relay, because QwenPaw skips auth for 127.0.0.1/::1 and an in-container proxy would silently disable the login (measured 200 via relay vs 401 direct). Rebinding to :: was tried and reverted, since Python sets IPV6_V6ONLY and Railway's public proxy is IPv4. Auto-registration variables are write-once, and so is the agent language: upstream defaults to zh with no env var and no init flag, and its non-interactive init copies persona files with skip_existing=True, so the wrapper seeds config.json and runs init itself before handing off (QWENPAW_AGENT_LANGUAGE, default en). Apache-2.0. See guide §5.31.

### ReMe — 🟠 review

Template repo [RockinPaul/reme_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/reme_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/reme)

> First template with NO upstream image (ReMe ships only a pip package) and NO upstream authentication - upstream states the service layer has none, CORS allows any origin, jobs may write/move/delete files, and instructs users to place it behind an authenticated reverse proxy rather than exposing it directly. So Caddy holds a generated bcrypt credential as the only public listener while ReMe keeps its default 127.0.0.1 bind and service.host=0.0.0.0 is never set. All 149 resolved packages are hash-pinned in requirements.lock because without an upstream image a rebuild is a fresh resolution; regenerate on linux/amd64. reme-ai declares reme_studio with no version constraint, so the lock is what stops the UI drifting. Apache-2.0. See guide §5.32.

### Trivy Server — 🟠 review

Template repo [RockinPaul/trivy_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/trivy_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/trivy-server)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image, used unmodified. Adds Railway environment plus a guard that refuses to start without TRIVY_TOKEN, because an empty token disables authentication entirely and upstream's own guidance for an exposed server is to configure one. No browser surface; the listing leads with the client command. Image and git tags are both plain semver with a v prefix on the git side. Apache-2.0. See guide \u00a75.30.

### PaddleOCR — 🟡 minor drift

Template repo [RockinPaul/paddleocr_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/paddleocr_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/paddleocr)

> Second template with no upstream image (PaddleOCR ships pip packages only) and no upstream authentication, so Caddy holds a generated credential and paddlex --serve is pinned to 127.0.0.1 rather than its 0.0.0.0 default. FIRST template with NO VOLUME: stateless, and the pipeline's 5 models (177 MB) are baked into the image because PaddleX downloads them when the SERVER starts, not lazily. The first inference then costs 98s versus ~4s warm, so the entrypoint runs a throwaway inference before the proxy opens. PADDLE_PDX_ENABLE_MKLDNN_BYDEFAULT=False is REQUIRED: oneDNN breaks every request on real x86 with PaddlePaddle 3.3.1, and a local emulated rehearsal cannot reproduce it. Apache-2.0. See guide §5.33.
